# Catalogue import report, first pass

mordheimer's items plus our MiM list, cleaned into `out/`. Generated by `build.py`; do not edit by hand.
54 shared rules, 43 of them worded differently on some items (those items keep their wording as a text override).

## Start here

1. **Same-name list:** 6 names appear more than once across mordheimer and MiM. mordheimer's entry is the main one; the PDF pass settles each MiM twin (below).
2. **Warband groups:** 0 membership questions left; overrides.json settles where 35 types stand (the table below). Good, Evil and Mercenaries come from mordheimer's hiring lists, so the 86 types mordheimer lacks get their sides in the hired-swords pass.
3. **Items no one may buy:** 14 mordheimer items exclude every warband and permit none, mostly Khemri's; 14 of them settled in overrides.json (the contradictions table).
4. **mordheimer's contradictions:** 45 places where an item's lists and its own notes disagree; 45 settled in overrides.json, 0 left at the default taken.
5. **Warband names:** 15 names in mordheimer's data match no warband type, or more than one; 5 settled in overrides.json (Amazons, Chaos Dwarfs, Mercenaries, Night Goblins, Norse Explorers), the rest each name one type.
6. **Settings for our skills and hired swords:** 27 names mordheimer gives more than one setting and 1 entry it lists in several; 28 of the 28 settled in overrides.json.
7. **Who can hire and learn:** 0 hired swords, dramatis personae and skills name a warband no type matches; 26 fees aren't plain numbers, 0 of them unread.

Answers go into `overrides.json`; the next build applies them.

## Broheim and mordheimer grades

No conflicts: wherever both sites grade a warband they agree (mordheimer's "core" is 1a). Note that Norse Explorers appears twice in Broheim (Town Cryer 13, 1b; Border Town Burning, 1c) and both rows point at one mordheimer page.

## Settings for the catalogue we have

Items get theirs in the items rebuild. A row is fixed in overrides.json "setting_assignments" by kind and by the name shown here.

| dramatis personae | 56 | 30 | 26 | 0 |

## Who can hire and learn

`out/who-can.json`: mordheimer's `permittedWarbands` for its hired swords and dramatis personae (by name), as warband types or groups. Our skill rows are read the same way (from the entry each takes its setting from; a row mordheimer lacks is for everyone on a general list, else for the warband its list is named after), and each skill group's who-can is built from them (below).

| Kind | Entries | Everyone | Everyone except | Named warbands |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| hired swords | 72 | 0 | 0 | 72 |
| dramatis personae | 30 | 1 | 0 | 29 |

Skills mordheimer lacks, read from their list's name: Heron Strike (Battle Monks of Cathay) -> Battle Monks of Cathay; Swallow Kick (Battle Monks of Cathay) -> Battle Monks of Cathay; Water Form (Battle Monks of Cathay) -> Battle Monks of Cathay; Inch Punch (Battle Monks of Cathay) -> Battle Monks of Cathay; Virtue of the Impetuous (Bretonnian Knights) -> Bretonnian Knights; Virtue of the Squire (Bretonnian Knights) -> Bretonnian Knights; Animosity (Forest Goblins) -> Forest Goblins; Tea-leaf (Militant Mootlanders) -> Militant Mootlanders; Flan-eater (Militant Mootlanders) -> Militant Mootlanders; Looter (Militant Mootlanders) -> Militant Mootlanders; Backstabber (Low Kings) -> Low Kings; Pick Locks (Low Kings) -> Low Kings; Slaver (Low Kings) -> Low Kings; Cutpurse (Low Kings) -> Low Kings; Infiltrate (Night Goblins) -> Night Goblins; ’ard ’ead (Orcs) -> Orcs; Luck (Sea Ghosts) -> Sea Ghosts.

Fees that aren't plain numbers, as structured fees (kind, gold, D6, resources and a note for the rest). ## Skill lists (William's decisions)

`overrides.json` (`skill_lists`, `drop_skills`, `extra_skills`, `skill_descriptions`) applied to our skill rows before they become skill groups (below). A list's decision is its group's: who learns from it, its note shown when learning, and a Large unit's group warns for any other unit, never blocks.

| List | Learned by | Note | Skills it places |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Animal | everyone |  | [Animal] Handler, Ride [Animal], Falconer |
| Large Special | everyone, Large units only |  | Seismic Toss, Juggernaut, Brick Wall, Frog Splash, Titanic Grip |
| Skaven Metal Mongers | Metal Mongers |  | Unblinking Eye, Metallic Body, Respirator, Tinker-Rat |
| Undead Vampires | Undead | Vampire only | Transfixing Glare, Mist Form, Ratswarm Form, Bat Form, Giant Wolf Form |
| Pirates (TBMF) | Pirates |  | Captain Blood, Treasure Hunter |
| Dwarf Exiled Engineers | Guild of Disgraced Engineers |  | Tunneler, Tinkerer, Quick Thinker, Master Inventor |

## Skill groups

`out/skills.json` is 510 skills, one per name; `out/skill-groups.json` is 114 groups holding them (660 links), the five core groups first.

### Merged by name

16 names sat in two or more lists; each is one skill now, in every group that had it, under the title-case spelling. The wording is overrides.json's (`skill_wordings`) or the longest; every other wording is dropped:

| Skill | Rows merged | Wording | Dropped wordings |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Powerful Build | Powerful Build (Dark Elf), Powerful Build (Sea Ghosts), Powerful Build (Shadow Warriors) | longest | Powerful Build (Dark Elf): The warrior is strongly built for an Elf and is capable of feats of strength. A warrior with this skill may choose skills from the Strength table. The Sorceress may never take this skill and no more than two warriors in the warband may take this skill at any one time. (TC10) / Powerful Build (Sea Ghosts): The warrior is strongly built for an Elf, and is capable of feats of strength not often seen among the elder race. A warrior with this skill may choose skills from the Strength skills table from now on. There may never be more than two Elves with this skill in the warband at any one time. (MiM) |
| Infiltration | Infiltration (Dark Elf), Infiltration (Sea Ghosts), Infiltration (Skaven), Infiltration (Skaven Metal Mongers) | overrides.json | Infiltration (Dark Elf): A Skaven with this skill is always placed on the battlefield after the opposing warband and can be placed anywhere on the table as long as it is out of sight of the opposing warband and more than 12" away from any enemy model. If both players have models which infiltrate, roll a D6 for each, and the lowest roll sets up first. (RB 93) / Infiltration (Sea Ghosts): An Elf with this skill is always placed on the battlefield after the opposing warband and can be placed anywhere on the table as long as it is out of sight of the opposing warband and more than 12" away from any enemy model. If both players have models which infiltrate, roll a D6 for each, and the lowest roll sets up first. (MiM) / Infiltration (Skaven Metal Mongers): A Skaven with this skill is always placed on the battlefield after the opposing warband and can be placed anywhere on the table as long as it is out of sight of the opposing warband and more than 12" away from any enemy model. If both players have models that infiltrate, roll a D6 for each, and the lowest roll sets up first. (MiM) |
| Master of Blades | Master of Blades (Dwarves), Master of Blades (Dwarf Troll Slayers) | longest | - |
| Extra Tough | Extra Tough (Dwarves), Extra Tough (Dwarf Exiled Engineers), Extra Tough (Dwarf Troll Slayers) | longest | Extra Tough (Dwarf Exiled Engineers): This Dwarf is notorious for walking away from wounds that would kill a lesser being. When rolling on the Heroes Serious Injury Chart for this hero after a game in which he has been taken Out of Action, the dice may be re-rolled once. The result of this second dice roll must be accepted, even if it is a worse result. (MiM) |
| Resource Hunter | Resource Hunter (Dwarves), Resource Hunter (Dwarf Troll Slayers) | longest | - |
| True Grit | True Grit (Dwarves), True Grit (Dwarf Exiled Engineers), True Grit (Dwarf Troll Slayers) | longest | True Grit (Dwarf Exiled Engineers): Dwarfs are hardy individuals and this hero is hardy even for a Dwarf! When rolling on the Injury Table for this hero, a roll of 1-3 is treated as Knocked Down, 4- 5 as Stunned and 6 is Taken Out. (MiM) |
| Thick Skull | Thick Skull (Dwarves), Thick Skull (Dwarf Exiled Engineers), Thick Skull (Dwarf Troll Slayers) | longest | Thick Skull (Dwarves): The Hero has a thick skull, even for a Dwarf. He has a 3+ save on a D6 to avoid being stunned. If the save is made, treat a stunned result as knocked down instead. If the Dwarf also wears a helmet, this save is 2+ instead of 3+ (this takes the place of the normal Helmet special rule). (A02 24) |
| Hide in Shadows | Hide in Shadows (Night Goblins), Hide in shadows (Hochland Bandits), Hide in Shadows (Sea Ghosts), Hide in Shadows (Shadow Warriors) | overrides.json | Hide in Shadows (Night Goblins): The sneaky Goblin has become an expert at concealing himself from his enemies (and potential victims!). An enemy warrior attempting to detect this warrior when he is Hidden must halve his Initiative (round up) before measuring the distance. (TBMF) / Hide in shadows (Hochland Bandits): The Bandit has become an expert at concealing himself from his enemies (and potential victims!). An enemy warrior attempting to detect this warrior when he is Hidden must halve his Initiative (round up) before measuring the distance. (NC) / Hide in Shadows (Sea Ghosts): Over time the Hero has learned how to freeze in place and remain undetected, even from the heightened senses of their Dark Elf cousins. An enemy warrior attempting to detect this warrior when he is Hidden must halve his Initiative before measuring the distance. (MiM) / Hide in Shadows (Shadow Warriors): Over time Shadow Warriors have learned how to freeze in place and remain undetected, even from the heightened senses of their Dark Elf cousins. An enemy warrior attempting to detect this warrior when he is Hidden must halve his Initiative before measuring the distance. (TC10) |
| Crude Belch | Crude belch (Militant Mootlanders), Crude belch (Ogres) | longest | Crude belch (Ogres): Ogres eat almost anything. Consequences are to be expected from those inconsiderate enough to consume a rich meal before battle. A Hero with this 'condition' may unleash his thunderous fumes on all enemies engaged in close combat. Those that do not pass a Ld test suffer a –1 ‘to hit’ modifier for the turn. The Ogre must wait until a new enemy engages him in combat before he relieves himself again. (BTB) |
| Stealthy | Stealthy (Militant Mootlanders), Stealthy (Low Kings) | longest | Stealthy (Low Kings): The Hero can hide even after running, and can run while within 8" of enemy models if he starts and ends his move hidden. (MiM) |
| Battle Tongue | Battle tongue (Norse), Battle Tongue (Academic) | overrides.json | Battle tongue (Norse): Only a hero with the leader skill may gain this skill. It allows models within 12” of him to use bis leadership, rather than the normal 6”. (TC 13) |
| Cutlass Master | Cutlass Master (Pirates), Cutlass Master (Pirates (TBMF)) | longest | Cutlass Master (Pirates (TBMF)): These short, single bladed swords are the mainstay weapon of any pirate crew, and in the hands of a trained seaman they are superb weapons for close quarters fighting. If the pirate is equipped with a Sword, this skill will give him the additional benefit of also being able to parry successfully if the player rolls equal to the number rolled to hit, not just higher as normal. (FO 65) |
| Swashbuckler | Swashbuckler (Pirates), Swashbuckler (Pirates (TBMF)) | longest | Swashbuckler (Pirates): The pirate cuts a dashing figure in combat, mixing dazzling swordplay and acrobatic feats with charm and witty comments. Even the basest villains in Mordheim respect (and curse) his ability to always seem to effortlessly slip from their grasp. The pirate may make a Leadership Test at the end of any Hand-to-Hand phase (pirate’s or enemy’s turn) if he is still in base contact with any enemy models. If he passes he may make a normal movement away from the enemy (he may not run or charge), without the enemy striking any blows on him. If he fails the test he remains in combat and must fight as normal in the following turn. (TC 9) |
| See in Shadows | See in Shadows (Sea Ghosts), See in Shadows (Shadow Warriors) | longest | See in Shadows (Sea Ghosts): The warrior's senses have become especially keen from years spent walking the shadows. As long as he has movement to reach them, the warrior may always roll to charge opponents he cannot see (instead of the normal 4"). (MiM) |
| Black Hunger | Black Hunger (Skaven), Black Hunger (Skaven Clan Pestilens) | longest | - |
| Tail Fighting | Tail Fighting (Skaven), Tail Fighting (Skaven Metal Mongers) | longest | Tail Fighting (Skaven Metal Mongers): The Skaven may wield a shield, dagger or a sword with its tail. The model gains an extra attack with the appropriate weapon or a +1 bonus to its armour save. (MiM) |

### Warband rules

- Applied as data: Reikland's Marksmen BS +1, Middenheim's Mercenary Captain and Champions S +1 (S4), Remas' Captain, Champions and Youngbloods Ld +1, Marienburg's 600 gc, the Hung's 12 warriors and the Kurgan's any number of warhounds; the Ostermarkers' rules are for their type.
- Applied as data (phase 3): Marienburg's and the Norse' +1 and the Kurgan's -1 to find rare items (not for Great Axes and Barbed Whips), where the store's rare search starts its bonus; the Kurgan's 10 gc bows on the heroes' and henchmen's lists and the Hung's 40 gc warhorses on the heroes' list, for that variant only; Ride [Animal] for every Hung hero.
- Not applied (rules the data can't hold): Trantio's extra 100 gc in a one-off game and 20% in a Lustrian campaign (it depends on the campaign), Miragliano's +1 to hit with crossbows (heroes) and missiles (Marksmen), Remas' rout re-roll against Dark Elves, a Hung henchman who becomes a hero getting Ride Warhorse, and the Ostermarker captain's starting wardog.
- Mercenaries: the Ostermarkers pick one of the three cities' skill tables.
- mercenaries: 'Ostermarker Special rules' (heading Ostermark) is for the ostermarkers type only.
- A rule worded for another fighter only ("Pit Veterans have") takes the first fighter's wording; one that really differs is named apart, with its fighter or upgrade group: black-orcs: Black Orc (Black Orc); ## Shared specials

- Cloud of Flies: Carnival of Chaos: Plague Bearer; Carnival of Chaos: Nurgling; Carnival of Chaos: Blessings of Nurgle; Marauders of Chaos: Blessings of Nurgle; Fen Guard: Variations; Underworld Alliance: Warpstone for Breakfast; Plaguebearer.
- Bloated Foulness: Carnival of Chaos: Blessings of Nurgle; Marauders of Chaos: Blessings of Nurgle.
- Mark of Nurgle: Carnival of Chaos: Blessings of Nurgle.
- Suckers: Marauders of Chaos: Blessings of Nurgle; Shallows Beasts: Aquatic Mutants; Underworld Alliance: Warpstone for Breakfast.

| Immune to Psychology (Nightmare) | Nightmare | Never takes Leadership tests, and stands still if left leaderless. |
| Immune to Poison (Plague Bearers) | Carnival of Chaos: Plague Bearer | Immune to diseases too: a Disease Dagger's spares only the Undead and the Possessed. |
| Immune to Poison (Nurglings) | Carnival of Chaos: Nurgling | Immune to diseases too: a Disease Dagger's spares only the Undead and the Possessed. |
| Regeneration (Troll) | Night Goblins (web): Troll; Night Goblins: Troll; Orc Mob: Troll; Night Goblins (Karak Azgal): Troll | Ignores each wound on a 4+, never one from fire, and never rolls for injury; the mutation heals once. |
| Regeneration (Black Orcs' Troll) | Black Orcs: Troll | The Trolls' rule, but one taken out of action by fire rolls for injury as a henchman. |
| Regeneration (Belandysh) | Belandysh, Condemned Champion of Chen | Ignores each wound on a 4+, never one from fire; the mutation heals once. |
| Scaly Skin (Cold One Beasthounds) | Dark Elves: Cold One Beasthound | A 6+ armour save; the mutation's is 5+, and armour adds to it. |
| Scaly Skin (Kroxigor) | Lizardmen: Kroxigor | A natural 4+ save; the mutation's is 5+. |
| Scaly Skin (Chameleon Skink) | Chameleon Skink | The Lizardmen's Scaly Skin, a Skink's 6+; the mutation's is 5+. |
| No Pain (Sigmund) | Sigmund Spindle, the Harvester of Flesh | Ignores knocked down results too: only losing his last wound takes him out of action. |
| No Pain (Veskit) | Veskit, High Executioner of Clan Eshin | Ignores knocked down results too: only losing his last wound takes him out of action. |
| Agile (Fighting Ape) | The Cursed Cavalcade: Fighting Ape | The Scale Sheer Surfaces, Acrobat and Dodge skills and a diving charge from up to 10" high; the Grace of Shornaal is +1 to Initiative tests. |
| Mer-creature (Fishmen) | Shallows Beasts: Fishman | No Movement penalty on land; the Favour of Tchar is -1 Movement there. |
| Regeneration (Boglar) | Underworld Alliance: Boglar | Each wound ignored on a 5+, never against fire; the Bounty of Arkhar heals every wound on a 4+, once. |
| Regeneration (Warpstone Troll) | Underworld Alliance: Warpstone Troll | Each wound ignored on a 4+, never against fire; the Bounty of Arkhar heals every wound on a 4+, once, and the Trolls never roll for injury. |
| Savage (Orc Nutta) | Black Orcs: Orc Nutta | He must always run or charge his full distance at the nearest enemy he can see, fights with an extra attack outside his profile and his racial maximum, may never use armour or missile weapons and may never lead or lend his Leadership to a rout test: the Savage Orcs' Savage, which is frenzy and nothing else, is the shared Frenzy. |
| Berserkers (Red Toof Goblin) | Forest Goblins: Red Toof Goblin | Frenzy, and immune to animosity in a turn he begins within charge range of an enemy. |
| Kindred Hatred (Strigoi Vampire) | Survivors of Strigos: Strigoi Vampire | Hates vampires of every other bloodline, not High Elves. |
| Stupidity (Black sheep) | Mazzalupo: Black sheep | Not stupid while a Sheepherder is within 6". |
| Hatred | Bretonnian Chapel Guard: Battle Pilgrim | Hates every enemy: an open-ended target no shared row names, kept his own and worded short. |
| Loner (Hobgoblin Scout) | Hobgoblin Scout | May never use the warband leader's Leadership for any test, and operates independently. |
| Loner (Wildkin) | Sea Ghosts: Wildkin | Can never be the warband's leader. |
| Loner (Mountain Guide) | Maneaters: Mountain Guide | Never claims a Gnoblar or takes one as a pet, and may never become the warband leader. |
| Loner (Shinobi) | Nipponese Expedition: Shinobi | Only that he may never become the warband's leader: his rule grants no All Alone immunity at all. |
| Loner (Body Snatcher) | Body Snatcher | Always strikes first while he is all alone: his page reuses the name for a different rule. |

### Own rules of one name

### Adventurers

- Flag (p19): The warband's minimum of 4 models is not imported: our templates hold no minimum.
- Flag (p19): "The maximum amount of Heroes in your warband is 6" is not imported as a rule: the six Hero entries are each 0-1, and the sixth (the Imperial Captain) is not a fighter row of ours.
- Flag (p19): The Imperial Captain (0-1) has no profile and no hire cost printed: he exists only as a promoted Cannon Fodder, so no fighter row was made. His terms are in the Cannon Fodder's Mixed Bunch rule.
- Flag (p19): The leader rule numbers the Heroes 1 to 5 and rolls a D6 before each battle, but does not say what a 6 means. It is written as the warband possibly having no leader that battle, which is what the PDF says follows from the Captain never leading.
- Flag (p20): The Elf's entry calls his list the "Ranger Equipment list"; the list on p20 is headed "Elf Equipment List". Read as one list.
- Flag (p20): The Dwarf list prints Sword with no price. It is imported at 10 gc, the price the same PDF's other lists give a Sword.
- Flag (p20): The Barbarian list holds only Flail, Double Handed Weapon and Morning Star: no dagger and no armour, which matches his "will never wear armour" rule.
- Flag (p20): The Dwarf list's "Gromril Weapon 3x the cost" links our Gromril Weapon, priced 3 × price as the Slayer Cult's is.
- Flag (p22): The Barbarian's Leadership is printed "10/7": 10 is taken as his Leadership and the 7 is his Dim-witted rule, which is how the rule reads. Ld 10 is above our Human maximum of 9, so the app will warn.
- Flag (p22): The Elf's "Fey Quickness. Same as TC12" is linked to our Fey Quickness, whose text cites TC10.
- Flag (p22): The Barbarian's Ferocious Charge (double attacks on the charge, -1 to hit) is word for word our Ferocious Charge, so it is linked; the Dwarf's cites the rulebook and is the same skill.
- Flag (p23): The Noble's Taunt is not our Taunt (the Ostlander's shooting-phase insult), so it is kept apart as Taunt (Adventurers). His Trading Flair overlaps our Haggle but adds the rare-item re-roll and the 5+ condition, so it is a new skill. See the decisions.
- Flag (p23): The Dwarf's Hard to Kill and Hard Head cite the rulebook; they are written in the words our other Dwarf warbands use. The Barbarian's special skill Hard to Kill has the same effect and is a new skill of that name.
- Flag (p23): Magic Resistant is the Cursed Dwarves' skill too: one skill with the same words in both groups, so "the Dwarf only" sits in this group's note.
- Flag (p21): Only the Elf, Barbarian, Imperial Noble and Dwarf have a special skill table; the Wizard does not, so he does not tick Special.
- Flag (p21): The profile row is headed "Elf Ranger"; the fighter entry is "0-1 Elf", which is the name imported.
- Flag (p20): Heroes start with 8 experience and Cannon Fodder with 0. No group sizes are printed for the Cannon Fodder, and no maximum.

### Albion Barbarians

- Alias (p3): "double handed weapon" is our Double-handed weapon.
- Flag (p2): The warband's minimum of 3 models is not imported: our templates hold no minimum.
- Flag (p3): The PDF prints one equipment list for the whole warband and says the Warriors "use all kinds of weapons the tribe produces except bows", so the Bow line is read as closed to Warriors; every other line is open to all.
- Flag (p3): The list prints its Armor section with a Shield only. No other armour is sold.
- Flag (p4): Starting experience: Chief 20, Shaman 8, Champions 4, Henchmen 0.
- Flag (p5): Savage Fury is our Savage Fury, the Amazons' (+1 Attack when charging, immune to charm effects and fear), and merged into it (William, 12 Nov 2026: "keep as 1 add charm"): this group learns ours, which is reworded away from the Amazon, and this page's limit of the bonus to the first round of combat is dropped.
- Flag (p5): Concealment is kept apart as Concealment (Albion Barbarians): ours halves the spotting range in jungle terrain only and does not let him shoot while hidden. See the decisions.
- Flag (p5): The six Shaman spells are a new lore, Barbarian Shaman Magic, with the difficulties the PDF prints (6, 6, 7, 7, 5, 7).

### Altdorf Expeditionary Party

- Stub: Glyph of Blasting, 25 gc, Rare 10; its rules are on p5.
- Stub: Grimoire of Magic, 200+1D6x50 gc, Rare 13; its rules are on p6.
- Flag (p1): The warband's minimum of 3 models is not imported: our templates hold no minimum.
- Flag (p1): "50% Students: A maximum of half your warband may be Students" is a warband rule: our fighter counts are whole numbers.
- Flag (p1): The skill table was read from the page as an image (its ticks are glyphs): Arch-Mage Academic, Speed, Special; Men-At-Arms Combat, Shooting, Strength, Speed, Special; the Mage ticks Special and two lists of the player's choice.
- Flag (p2): The Mage has no profile ("N/A") and no hire cost: he exists only as a promoted Student, so no fighter row was made, as the Karak Azgal Adventurers' Imperial Captain has none. His terms are in the Student's Very Talented rule.
- Flag (p2): The Student's Wizard rule says he counts as a wizard but "being Henchmen, they still cannot use any equipment", while his entry says he picks weapons and armour from the Wizard list. Both are kept as printed; he buys from the Wizard list.
- Flag (p2): Starting experience: Arch-Mage 20, Men-At-Arms 8. None is printed for the henchmen, so they start with 0.
- Flag (p3): The Soldier's entry says "1 spear, halberd, or sword" while the list's asterisks mark Dagger, Sword, Spear, Toughened Leathers, Light armour, Heavy armour, Shield and Helmet as his, with no asterisk on the Halberd. The asterisks are followed, because they also settle his armour; the Halberd is closed to Soldiers. See the decisions.
- Flag (p3): The Halberd is 15 gc on the Wizard list and 10 gc on the Warrior list. Both are kept as printed.
- Flag (p3): The Wizard list's Wand/Staff/Mace is our Club, Mace or Hammer at the same 3 gc, shown under the PDF's name; so is the Warrior list's Hammer/Mace.
- Flag (p4): The Familiar is our Familiar: 20 gc on both, and ours is the same ritual and re-roll. Ours re-rolls one failed casting roll each turn where the PDF's does it once per game, and the PDF prints "Ld" where its rarity would go, which we cannot read, so ours (Rare 8) stands. See the decisions.
- Flag (p4): The Quarterstaff is "just a mace" (William, 12 Nov 2026): 3 gc with Concussion, which is our Club, Mace or Hammer word for word and price for price, so the line buys ours, shown as Quarterstaff. Its Two-handed and Parry are not carried, and our Quarter Staff (15 gc, Balanced and Freestyle) is a different weapon and untouched. The Wizard list sells our Club, Mace or Hammer twice, once as Wand/Staff/Mace and once as Quarterstaff, as the page does.
- Flag (p5): The Glyph of Blasting is a stub typed Miscellaneous, not a weapon: it is inscribed on a weapon and is not wielded itself. Its Arcane bolt is part of its rules, not a spell of a lore.
- Flag (p6): The Grimoire of Magic is printed "(Optional)", Rare 13, 200 + D6x50 gc, and no equipment list sells it, so it is a stub with a price row of its own for this warband.
- Flag (p5): The PDF says the Familiar, Quarterstaff, Glyph of Blasting and Grimoire of Magic "are available to all warbands for any warrior that can use them". The Quarterstaff is our Club, Mace or Hammer, which everyone buys already; the Glyph of Blasting and the Grimoire of Magic keep their rows for this warband alone (William, 12 Nov 2026), so another warband sees them as not usually available, which warns rather than blocks.
- Flag (p7): Each special skill names who may take it beyond this warband ("may be taken by any College wizard", "any wizard or prayer user"). The limit within the warband opens the skill's description; the grant to other warbands is not imported (William, 11 Nov 2026).
- Flag (p7): Dispell is kept apart as Dispell (Altdorf Expeditionary Party): our Dispel needs a roll higher than the casting roll, where the PDF's dispels on equal or higher, needs line of sight and allows one attempt per casting. See the decisions.
- Flag (p7): The Large Familiar skill also lets a campaign using expanded mount rules take any mount's profile for half that mount's price. That is left out: it points at rules we do not hold. The wardog and giant rat profiles and their 25 gc and 15 gc are kept.
- Flag (p8): Common Magic is a new lore of six spells, difficulties 9, 9, 10, 7, 9 and 9. The eight Colleges change what those spells do and are one warband rule; the player picks a Mage's College.

- Flag (p2): "Axe (Katar)" at 5 gc is read as our Axe shown under the list's name; we also hold a Katar of our own (5 gc, Rare 4), which this line is not: see the decisions.

- Flag (p1): The Pirate column is the Pirate Skills list of the Sartosa campaign rules (Sartosa Skills, Hired Swords and Dramatis Personae, p1-2), not this PDF: it is the shared Sartosa Pirate Skills group, declared in sartosan-pirates.json and named by each hero who ticks it.

### Arabyan Nomads

- Alias (p3): "jambiya" is our dagger.
- Alias (p3): "light armor" is our light armour.
- Alias (p3): "heavy armor" is our heavy armour.
- Stub: Ankus, 13 gc, Common; its rules are on p1.
- Stub: Cutlass, 15 gc, Common; its rules are on p1.
- Stub: Great Scimitar, 20 gc, Common; its rules are on p1.
- Stub: Scimitar, 15 gc, Common; its rules are on p1.
- Stub: Scythe (Khemri), 5 gc, Common; its rules are on p1.
- Stub: Bagh Nakh, , Common; its rules are on p1.
- Flag (p1): The PDF prints no starting gold, only "Maximum warband number is 15". The template carries our 500 gc default.
- Flag (p2): The Champion's profile reads S4 for a human champion; as printed.
- Flag (p2): The Street Mage "starts with one Elemental Spell, whichever element they chose is the only one they can get". Khemri's own Elemental Magic PDF (read 12 Nov 2026) holds four lists of six spells - Ice, Fire, Wind and Earth - so he is now given Khemri's Ice Magic, Fire Magic, Wind Magic and Earth Magic in place of our four Elemental Lores (Air, Earth, Fire, Water), which are the Sorcerous Society's Mordheim 2a lores. The one-element limit stays in his Wizard rule.
- Flag (p3): The Khemri Equipment PDF (https://www.broheim.net/downloads/campaigns/khemri/Equipment.pdf, read 11 Nov 2026 at William's direction) gives these weapons their rules, and this page declares them for the setting. The Jambiya is our Dagger, "just a nicknamed dagger" (William), whose +1 enemy armour save is the Jambiya's rule. The Katar is ours already, the same -1 enemy armour save. "Scimitar" at 15 gc is its own item now (as user, Parry, Cutting edge, where our Sword parries only), so the tag that made this list sell our Sword twice is dropped. The Scythe is Scythe (Khemri) (as user, Cutting edge, Two Handed), kept apart from the Sylvania scythes (William: "in this list scythe is something unique so give it the 'khemri' treatment"). Ankus (as user, Strike first only when charged, Concussion), Cutlass (as user, Parry, an extra punch attack when a hit is successful) and Great Scimitar (+2, Two-handed, Strike last, Cutting edge) now carry their rules. The Bagh Nakh (+1, -1 enemy armour save, pair, cumbersome) is on the page but on no warband list and at no price: a row for the setting's warband types with no price. The page's Vanguards and Sword of Unholy Turning are not imported (William: "ignore both").
- Flag (p3): The Warrior list's Scythe is 5 gc and its Katar 5 gc. The Ankus is "used as a spear, when charged and a staff in hth", and its rules line prints "Strike first (only when charged)": read as striking first in the turn he charges. The Cutlass's extra punch attack is printed with no Strength of its own. The Khemri page's Tufenk is Strength 1 where our Tufenk (mordheimer, Khemri 1b) is Strength 2, and its Genie lamp is our Lamp of the Djinn with two table rows worded otherwise (its Good 4 and 5 are an extra hero and an extra henchman, where ours are a random and a chosen item, and its Bad 6 is nothing where ours is a roll on the injury chart) and a Jinn who fights for D3x10 gc, which ours does not carry: all three are for William, and nothing of ours was changed.
- Flag (p2): No group sizes are printed for the Warriors or the Slaves.
- Flag (p1): The Sheik and the Street Mage tick no Special column, so neither learns the warband's own skills.
- Flag (p2): Blending into the Sands (hide in open ground) is our Sand worm, the Tomb Robbers' skill of the same setting, and is merged into it (William, 12 Nov 2026): the group learns ours and the PDF's name is dropped.

### Arabyan Thieves

- Alias (p3): "jambiya" is our dagger.
- Alias (p3): "light armor" is our light armour.
- Alias (p3): "heavy armor" is our heavy armour.
- Stub: Ankus, 13 gc, Common; its rules are on p3.
- Stub: Cutlass, 15 gc, Common; its rules are on p3.

- Stub: Scimitar, 15 gc, Common; its rules are on p3.
- Flag (p1): The PDF prints no starting gold, only "Maximum warband number is 15". The template carries our 500 gc default.
- Flag (p1): Knowing the trade routes also says "All heroes in the thief warband may gain the Disarm/Spot trap skill". That is our Trap Expert, so it is a skill of the warband's own group (every Hero ticks Special) rather than a sentence in the warband rule. Khemri's Campaign Rules (https://www.broheim.net/downloads/campaigns/khemri/Khemri%20Campaign%20Rules.pdf, 1-22, text layer; read 12 Nov 2026) print the skill in full on p8, as Spot/Disarm trap and Set Trap: ours already held both halves with the same rolls and lacked only the limit of four takes, now in its description. See the decisions.
- Flag (p1): Known thieves: a captured thief sells for "1D6+ the model exprience", read as 1D6 gold crowns plus his experience. The chopped-off hand on a 1-3 is given no effect in the PDF; as printed.
- Flag (p1): The PDF's Hide in Shadows (an Initiative test to charge him while he is within 1" of a wall, said to be the Imperial Assassin's skill) and Knife fighting (+1 WS and +1 to injury with knives) are not what our skills of those names do, so each is a new skill named apart. See the decisions.
- Flag (p3): The Scimitar (15 gc) and the Scythe (5 gc) are their own Khemri items now, with the rules the Khemri Equipment PDF prints; the Scythe is Scythe (Khemri), kept apart from the Sylvania scythes, and the Jambiya is our Dagger. The Ankus and the Cutlass carry their rules too. All of them are declared on arabyan-nomads.json, the first page of the setting to sell them.
- Flag (p2): No group sizes are printed for the Thieves or the Beggars, and the Beggars have no maximum.

- Flag (p2): The warband's minimum of 3 models is not imported: our templates hold no minimum. Its maximum of 12 is.
- Flag (p2): Starting experience: Shadowblade 20, Silent Hunter 12, Black Knife 8, Young Snout 0, henchmen 0.
- Flag (p3): The skill tables are printed as prose, not a table of ticks, and were read from the text.
- Flag (p3): The "Silent Hunter Only" block (Kill Knife, Short Spear, Body Armour) sits under the Assassin column, so those three lines are on the Assassin list, closed to everyone but the Silent Hunter.
- Flag (p3): "These prices are only at warband creation" (Black Venom, Assassin Bullets) needs no rule: our list prices are already open only until the warband's first battle.
- Flag (p3): Black Venom is our Dark Venom, which the warband's own Master of Poisons skill names by that name, shown under the PDF's wording at its 10 gc list price. Ours is 30 gc Rare 8 in the store. See the decisions.
- Flag (p3): Throwing Stars is our Throwing Knives/Stars/Axes (William, 11 Nov 2026: one entry for all of them), shown under the PDF's name.
- Flag (p4): The Shadowblade's Perfect Killer and Fear are our shared specials: the same words.
- Flag (p4): The PDF grants the warband no rulebook Skaven skills: its heroes tick Special only, so the new Assassin Squad group is its own and the Skaven and Skaven of Karak Azgal groups are untouched.
- Flag (p6): The Chain Axe, Wyrd Blade, Kill Knife, Body Armour, Short Spear, Assassin Bullets and Black Darts are stubs: none matches an item of ours. Each is Common, so its price row carries no rarity.
- Flag (p6): The Kill Knife "is treated like being at the users strength with a maximum of S4", read as the wielder's Strength to a maximum of 4.
- Flag (p7): The Black Darts are printed "S3 (S2 + dark venom, some creatures are immune to poison)", read as Strength 2 plus the venom's +1, so Strength 2 against a model immune to poison.
- Flag (p7): Master of Poisons is kept apart as Master of Poisons (Assassin Squad): ours makes D3-1 doses of Dark Venom only, the PDF's portions of dark venom or black lotus. The PDF prints "3D-1", which is no dice we use anywhere; it is written D3-1 (William, 12 Nov 2026).
- Flag (p7): Silent Strike is kept apart as Silent Strike (Assassin Squad): our skill of the name lets a warrior shoot while hidden, where this one charges out of hiding and back. See the decisions.
- Flag (p8): The warband's Infiltration is word for word our Infiltration, so it is linked.
- Flag (p8): The Development Progression and Designer Notes are not imported.

### Blood Dragons

- Flag (p1): A Blood Dragon warband needs at least 3 models: our templates hold no minimum, so it is not imported. No henchmen group sizes and no maximum profiles are printed.
- Flag (p1): The PDF opens with the author's warning that the warband is too powerful and suggests limiting the Vampire to one bloodline ability. It is a note on play, not a rule, and is not imported.
- Flag (p2): The skill table (p2) prints no Special column, so the warband has no skill group: the Vampire takes Combat, Academic, Strength and Speed, the Wight and the Dreg Combat and Strength.
- Flag (p4): The Wight (p4) takes our Undead race and the Grave Guard (p6) our Grave Guard race; the Skeleton Warrior (p5) and the Hell Hound (p6) are creatures with no race and no maximum profile. The Dreg (p4) is Human.
- Flag (p5): The Skeletons' (p5) and Grave Guards' (p6) Slow (they may not run, but may charge normally) is our shared May Not Run, under that name.
- Flag (p2): The Blood Dragon list's Warhorse (80 gc) and Barding (40 gc) are marked "*Vampire Only". The Undead list has no Warhorse or Barding, and the Blood Dragon list no missile weapons.
- Flag (p4): The Vampire's Summoner rule is his own (the undead collapse while he is out of action); our skill of that name raises the maximum warband size, which is what his Gift of Abhorash upgrade does. The upgrade gives the template's own special of that name, and neither touches the skill.
- Flag (p7): The seven bloodline abilities are the warband's upgrades, each the special it gives at its price (30 to 50 gc), the Vampire's alone, the second and later at double. One may be taken later in place of a skill advance, which no upgrade records: the group's own words, the warband's Bloodline Abilities rule, say so.
- Flag (p6): Grave Guards are "0-5*", the asterisk the footnote that a warband may never have more Grave Guards than Skeletons: the count is 0-5 and the footnote is the warband's Grave Guards rule.

### Bretonnian Brigands

- Flag (p1): A Brigand warband needs at least 3 models: our templates hold no minimum, so it is not imported. No henchmen group sizes and no maximum profiles are printed, so the warband takes our Human race.
- Flag (p2): The skill table prints no Special column, so the warband has no skill group.
- Flag (p2): The Rapier, Halberd and Lance are the list's "Fallen Noble Extra Equipment" column: read as Fallen Noble only. "(#) Denotes Heroes Only equipment" marks the Heavy Armour, Barding and Crossbow, and the whole Miscellaneous Equipment heading, so the Horse and Warhorse are heroes only too.
- Flag (p2): "Rapier (TC 7)" is our Rapier; the (TC 7) is the source the PDF cites, not part of the name. The Barding (60 gc), Horse (30 gc), Warhorse (60 gc) and Hunting Arrows (25 gc) are marked "*Starting Price Only": list prices are starting prices in the app, so no note is added.
- Flag (p2): The Brigand list's Dagger is 2 gc with no "first free"; the Poacher list's is "Dagger (first free)". As printed.
- Flag (p4): The Scarface's Horribly Scarred (he causes fear) is our shared Fear, under that name.
- Flag (p4): The Fallen Noble's Knights Feats rule grants him the Knights Errant's skill list, so he learns from the Knights Feats group (declared in the Bretonnian Knights-Errant file for both types) and the rule itself is not imported: who may take a skill is never a warband rule. The group's note says he is the only Brigand who learns them.
- Flag (p6): The Green Jack's Weapons/Armour line reads "A Likely Lad may be equipped from the Poacher equipment list": read as the Green Jack's, the only hero the PDF gives that list.
- Flag (p4): The Fallen Noble is "0-1 FALLEN NOBLE ... 45 gc each" and the Hard Men "35 gc each"; the prices are each warrior's.

### Bretonnian Grail Companions

- Alias (p2): "light armor" is our light armour.
- Alias (p2): "heavy armor" is our heavy armour.
- Stub: Longsword, 25 gc, Common; its rules are on p3.
- Stub: Holy Shield, 30 gc, Rare 7; its rules are on p3.

- Flag (p1): The manifest gives no setting; our type's is Mordheim 2a, assumed (setting_assumed). Grade 2a matches.
- Flag (p1): A Grail Companion warband needs at least 3 models: our templates hold no minimum, so it is not imported.
- Flag (p2): The maximum profiles differ by fighter: Companions' (M4 WS6 BS6 S4 T4 W3 I6 A4 Ld9) is our Human; the Grail Knight's (M8 WS8 BS2 S7 T6 W4 I9 A4 Ld10) is a new race, Grail Knight. The skill table and profiles were read from the PDF's layout text, with no page image.
- Flag (p3): The Longsword is Common, Knights only, 25 gc (Parry, Versatile); the Holy Shield Rare 7, 30 gc (+1 save against magic, else a shield); the Pegasus Rare 11, Grail Knight only, 110 gc (a flying warhorse). Stubs, their rules on p3. The Pegasus sits in a Special equipment list for the Grail Knight.
- Flag (p3): The Lance is 20 gc on the Knight list (ours is 40 gc): taken as printed.
- Flag (p4): The Grail Knight's profile is printed WS4 BS- I4 A1 W1, as low as a Knight of the Realm's, under a maximum of WS8: taken as printed. His warhorse's (M8 WS3 BS- S3 T- W1 I- A1, no Ld) is in Loyal Steed's text; the free warhorse itself isn't imported, templates give no starting items.
- Flag (p4): Lord's Boon's choice is printed "1 set of barding, light armor and holy shield, or heavy armor" (Grail Knight) and "1 free warhorse, suit of light armor and holy shield, or heavy armor" (Knights of the Realm): which items go together is unclear, so the texts keep the PDF's grouping. Text only; the player adds the items free.
- Flag (p4): Fearsome ("This model causes Fear") links our shared Fear.
- Flag (p5): The Damsel casts Grail Magic (p9), a new lore of six spells in spells.json; Virtuous gives no Special list to her (the skill table ticks none).
- Flag (p6): The Knight Virtues are the Special skills, a new group Grail Companions; rolled on 3D6 (the table is a warband rule). Virtue of Noble Disdain, Purity and Discipline do other things than the Bretonnian Knights' virtues of those names, so they are named apart. Virtue of the Impetuous Knight (+1D3 to his charge) is our Virtue of the Impetuous (+D3" when charging), the same idea by name. Virtue of Knightly Temper (double attacks on the charge at -1 to hit) does what our Ferocious Charge does, but is kept apart (William, 6 Nov 2026: "for Knightly Temper and Excellent sight keep both of those separate as they have a name that inspires a different idea behind it").
- Flag (p7): Hedge Knights are henchmen who may become Heroes; Battle Pilgrims and Hunters never may (Commoner). No group sizes are printed: left open.
- Flag (p8): The Grail Reliquae gains no experience; its race is the construct creature type (no maximum), as it isn't a living warrior. Its rule Holy Shield shares the item's name but is its own 5+ save. Large is our shared Large. Honored Dead, printed for it too, is the Grail Knight's rule only (one wording), as it is how a Grail Knight becomes one.

### Bretonnian Knights-Errant

- Flag (p2): A Knights Errant warband needs at least 3 models: our templates hold no minimum, so it is not imported. No group size and no maximum profile are printed, so the warband takes our Human race.
- Flag (p2): A Knight Errant starts with D6+7 experience: the template stores 7, and his Bretonnian Leader rule carries the roll. The highest roll leads, so no fighter is marked the leader and the template's checks warn that it has none.
- Flag (p3): The skill table's heading is printed twice, the second time over the Knights Feats list. The Knight ticks Combat, Strength, Speed and Special; his Special column is the Knights Feats group, new to us, which he names among his groups because the group is shared with the Bretonnian Brigands.
- Flag (p3): Sword Master is printed as "When using a sword the character may parry, with reroll at equal or higher": kept as printed, since the PDF doesn't say what is re-rolled.
- Flag (p3): The same four feats are the Bretonnian Brigands' Fallen Noble's list (his PDF, p4), so Knights Feats is one shared group, declared here for both warband types; the Brigands' Fallen Noble names it among his groups, and the group's note says he is the only Brigand who learns them.
- Flag (p3): The Knight list's "Upgrade Light Armour to Heavy armour +30 gc" is our Heavy Armour at 30 gc, shown under the PDF's wording. The list's Mace/Hammer is our Club, Mace or Hammer and its Battleaxe our Axe.
- Flag (p4): The Knight's light armour and shield at recruitment, and that he must buy a weapon other than a dagger, are his fixed kit: neither is on his list, which prints no price for them.
- Flag (p4): Knights Virtue (he never breaks or panics and takes no All Alone test) is kept his own rule: our shared Immune to Psychology says more.

The Corpse Master is Human, as the PDF says; the Revenants take our Undead.

- Flag (p2): The list prints the Bow at 15 gc and the Crossbow at 10 gc (ours 10 and 25), perhaps swapped, and Light Armour at 25 gc (ours 20): as printed. Its Dagger is "Free\2gc", read as 1st free/2.
- Flag (p2): The Spirit Knife is 25 gc on the list but 20 gc in its entry (p3): the line is 20, the entry's (William: "use 120" for the Fen Guard's Horn). Its starred "Ethereal Heroes only" is the Cairn Wraith, Tomb Banshee and Malignant Spirits. Its Spectral Touch names "a Spirit Dagger".
- Flag (p2): The Mortis Engine is 125 gc on the list but 195 gc in its entry (p3): the line is 195, the entry's. No rarity is printed: Common.
- Flag (p2): The Scythe prints no rules: it is the Vampire Hunters of Sylvania's Scythe stub, one item with a price row for each warband.
- Flag (p3): The Mortis Engine is a stub with its rules (Wagon our shared rule) and profile rows: Engine T6 W4, Requilary T6 W3 Ld7, Nightmare M6 T3 W1. The PDF doesn't say how the warband gets the corpses its rituals need. The Purple Wind's -2 is printed without a range: read with the +2's 12".
- Flag (p4): The Cairn Wraith's rules list no Leader, though he must lead the warband: he is given Leader (6").
- Flag (p4): His Wizard rule takes "one spell from the Necromancy list", its Zombies read as Revenants: the PDF's revised list (p6) is a new lore, Necromancy (Call of the Night Haint). Death Was Merely the Beginning: "rolls 11-15 Dead on the serious injury table" kept as printed.
- Flag (p4): Traumatic Passing: "takes an enemy OOA on a roll of a 6" doesn't say which roll; kept as printed. The Key and the Gate: "deploy the model 6\" from the Cairn Wraith" kept as printed (the Requilary's is within 6").
- Flag (p5): The Poltergeists' Cause Ruckus isn't named among their Special Rules but is printed under them: theirs. The Mourngul's Haunter of the Dark is "Hainter of the Dark" in its list of rules.
- Flag (p5): Revenants: Spectral Ascension is read as the henchman roll The Lad's Got Talent, so they gain experience and may be promoted.

Songster is the Slayer Cult's, one skill in both groups: its "Rememberer only" moves to the Slayer Cult's group note. The PDF's Songster has no "no more than 10". - Flag (p1): The seven construct rules (Need Orders, Immune to Psychology, No Pain, May Not Run, Immune to Poison, Brainless, In-built equipment) are printed once as a block. They are carried as each construct's own rules, so the four that are shared specials of ours link them, and the slot counts each fighter's entry prints sit in his In-built equipment.
- Flag (p2): The Clockwork pistol (25 gc) and Clockwork rifle (40 gc) have no rules in this PDF, and were read as our Pistol and our Handgun under the Clockworkers' names (William, 11 Nov 2026: "these are just nicknames"). The Sealed City's own Lost and Found (https://www.broheim.net/downloads/campaigns/sealedcity/Lost%20And%20Found.pdf, 1-5, text layer; read 12 Nov 2026) gives each its own entry - Clockwork pistol 8", Strength as user, Reload and Salvo; Clockwork rifle 18", Strength as user, Reload and Salvo - and its price chart sells the Clockwork pistol (25 gc Rare 8) beside the Pistol (15 gc Rare 9) and the Clockwork rifle (40 gc Rare 9) beside the Handgun (35 gc Rare 9), as four separate lines. They are different weapons, so each is now its own item at the chart's price and rarity, the two lists sell them under their own names and the Pistol and Handgun price rows they carried are gone. See the decisions.
- Flag (p2): The Heroes list prints Hammer and Staff at 3 gc each, both our Club, Mace or Hammer: carried as one line "Hammer/Staff". The Constructs list's Hammer and Mace, 3 gc each, are one line the same way.
- Flag (p2): The Constructs list is printed as 1-Slot and 2-Slot sections; our lists have no sections, so the slots are said in the list's caveat and in the In-built equipment rule.
- Flag (p6): The six Laws of Construction are carried as a new spell lore, Laws of Construction, with each law's difficulty, so the Master of Clocks can pick one. The PDF says a Law is not a spell: that is said in the warband rule of the same name.
- Flag (p3): Experimental enhancements rolls on the Stygian Mutation chart, which the setting's own Stygian Mutations PDF (https://www.broheim.net/downloads/campaigns/sealedcity/Stygian%20Mutations.pdf, 1-7, text layer; read 12 Nov 2026) prints. Its thirty D66 results are now specials: the ones that match ours link ours, the fourteen that differ or are new are the Sealed City's own mutations. The chart itself is not a table of ours. See the decisions.
- Flag (p3): Moonstones are spent by Gift of Sentience and Moonstone refinery. Lost and Found (read 12 Nov 2026) prices neither moonstones nor orichalcum tokens and is the only Sealed City document read, so the Orichalcum token stub still carries no rules and no moonstone item is made. See the decisions.
- Flag (p1): A Clockwork expedition needs at least 3 models: our templates hold no minimum, so it is not imported.
- Flag (p7): Page 7 is a scan with no text layer: it holds a picture and its caption only (opened as an image).

Each is declared here for the setting's five warband types, since the price chart is the setting's shop and no warband list sells most of them. The Krakensilver Weapon is "4 x Price" on the chart, so its row carries no figure.
- Flag (p4): Lost and Found p4-5 is the setting's price chart, read in place of the rulebook's. Every line but seven matches our own price and rarity, so only those seven take a row for the setting's types: Pike 12 gc Rare 9, Blunderbuss 30 gc Rare 10, Handgun 35 gc Rare 9, Hunting Rifle 200 gc Rare 12, Pistol 15 gc Rare 9, Barding 30 gc Rare 8 (ours is 80 gc) and Superior Blackpowder 30 gc Rare 12. The chart's Plate armour is 70 gc Rare 9 where the House Guard's own entry, already imported as the setting's item, is 70 gc Rare 7: ours is unchanged. Its River Boat is our Rowboat and its Superior Black powder our Superior Blackpowder. See the decisions.

### Dark Elf Corsairs

- Flag (p1): A Druchii Corsair warband needs at least 3 models: our templates hold no minimum, so it is not imported. No henchmen group sizes and no maximum profiles are printed.
- Flag (p1): The dark elves take the Elf (Druchii) race, the Druchii PDF's printed dark elf maximum (T3, where our Elf has T4); the PDF prints no maximum of its own. A decision for William.
- Flag (p2): The Reaver list's Repeating Crossbow and the Shades list's Repeater Crossbow are our Repeater Crossbow (ours 40 gc, the lists 35). The Dragon Cloak (50 gc) is our Sea Dragon Cloak, the same price, shown under this list's name (William, 12 Nov 2026: "corsairs should point at the other sea dragon cloak i think which is 50+2d6 in store, and starting price of just 50 on their list"). The +2/+1 stacking cloak the Druchii PDF prints at 30 gc is Sea Dragon Cloak (Druchii) and is not this warband's: that page's "Corsairs" is the Druchii's own henchman unit, whose rule reads "Corsairs may wear Sea Dragon Cloaks though they are not Heroes", not the Mousillon warband of the same name. This list's cloak therefore carries our rules, a flat 4+ save against missiles and 5+ in close combat, rather than the Druchii's +2 and +1.
- Flag (p6): Corsairs buy from the Reaver list "with the exception of Missile Weapons, of which they may have none": the Repeating Crossbow line is read as not Corsairs.
- Flag (p3): The four Specialists are hired swords in the catalogue, each hirable by the Dark Elf Corsairs alone (William, 11 Nov 2026: "why cant specialists just be hired swords that only that warband can hire?"): the Dark Elf Sorceress (70 gc to replace), the Dark Elf Beastmaster (110, his two Cold One Beasthounds his rule) and the Witch Elf (50), each hired for nothing with the tax and the replacement fine as his fees. Each is named for the warband, "Dark Elf Sorceress (Dark Elf Corsairs)" and so on, since only the Corsairs may hire him and the warband's name tells them apart (William, 11 Nov 2026); the Assassin is our Dark Elf Assassin, whose rules the PDF points at, with the Corsairs added to who may hire him. The warband rule keeps how a Specialist works and no longer lists them.
- Flag (p5): The Assassin Specialist's profile (M5 WS5 BS5 S4 T4 W1 I7 A1 Ld8) and "Same as Hired Sword from TC 12" are our Dark Elf Assassin, the same profile: no second row was made, and the Corsairs were added to who may hire him instead. His fees stay ours (70 gc and 25); the warband's Specialists rule carries the Black Ark's terms.
- Flag (p3): The Captain's Slaver rule is his own (it forces the sold-to-slavery option); our skill of that name captures a model automatically and is a different rule.
- Flag (p2): The Corsair special skills: Fey Quickness and Infiltration are ours, the same effects. Excellent Sight ("twice as far away as normal warriors") is the same skill the Druchii and the Wood Elves of Athel Loren groups hold, with their words, which William kept apart from our Unblinking Eye. Furious Charge (+1 Attack when charging) and Looting are new; our Looter adds a Treasure instead, so Looting is kept apart. A decision for William.
- Flag (p6): Shades' Elite (no Lad's Got Talent) makes them henchmen who gain experience but can't be promoted.

### Druchii

- Stub: Draich, 25+1D6 gc, Rare 8; its rules are on p7.
- Stub: Sea Dragon Cloak (Druchii), 30+2D6 gc, Rare 10; its rules are on p8.

- Flag (p1): Most headings (fighters', lists', skills' and items' names) are images the text layer lacks; each fighter was matched by the name in his own text, and pages 2, 3 and 8 were read as images for the skill table, the list titles and the skill names.
- Flag (p2): A Druchii warband needs at least 3 models: our templates hold no minimum, so it is not imported.
- Flag (p2): The Druchii maximum (M5 WS7 BS7 S4 T3 W3 I9 A4 Ld10) has T3 where our Elf has T4, so they take a new race, Elf (Druchii).
- Flag (p2): The skill table has no Strength column: no Druchii Hero learns Strength skills.
- Flag (p3): The Repeater crossbow is 20 gc on the lists (ours is 40 gc), a list price, which the app keeps to the starting warband. The Sea Dragon Cloak is 30 gc (ours 50 gc) and Dark Venom 15 gc: taken as printed.
- Flag (p3): The Darksteel blade is our Dark Elf Blade: the same Rare 9 and the same two rules, Critical Damage (+1 on the critical hit chart) and Wicked Edge (2-4 on the injury table is Stunned), so the two are one item, shown under this list's name (William, 11 Nov 2026: one entry for a weapon we already hold under another name). Its price is this list's "3 x price", in a price row for the Druchii; ours stays the Dark Elves' + 20 gc, Rare 9. Heroes only. The Draich is a stub at 25 gc (25+1D6, Rare 8, Strength as user +2, Two-handed, Swift), its rules on p7.
- Flag (p3): The Beastlash links our Beastlash at the list's 10 gc (the PDF prices it 10+D6, Rare 8).
- Flag (p6): Slavehounds come only with a Beastmaster (0-3, "if it also includes a Beastmaster"): our counts can't tie one fighter's to another's, so it is said in their Animals rule.
- Flag (p8): The PDF's Sea Dragon Cloak gives +2 to the save against shooting (5+ with none) and +1 in close combat (6+ with none) and may be worn with other armour with no penalty; ours is a flat 4+ save against missiles and 5+ in close combat. The two mechanisms really differ, so the PDF's is an item of its own, Sea Dragon Cloak (Druchii), at this list's 30+2D6, Rare 10, for this warband alone (William, 12 Nov 2026). Its brackets are this warband's, Mordheim 2a. The page's "Corsairs may wear Sea Dragon Cloaks though they are not Heroes" names this warband's own henchman unit, not the Mousillon Dark Elf Corsairs, who buy our standard cloak at their list's 50 gc.
- Flag (p8): The special skills are a new group, Druchii. Fey Quickness and Infiltration ("identical to the Skaven skill") are ours. Excellent Sight (hidden enemies spotted at twice his Initiative in inches) does what our Unblinking Eye does, but is kept apart (William, 6 Nov 2026: "keep both of those separate as they have a name that inspires a different idea behind it"); it is one skill with the Wood Elves' Excellent Sight. Fury of Khaine (ours adds no follow-up in the opponent's turn) and Master of Poisons (ours makes D3-1 doses, the PDF D2) are named apart. The blackletter heading reads "Vraithen Master", taken as printed.
- Flag (p9): Dark Magic differs from our Dark Elf Magic (Doombolt is Difficulty 8, ours 9; Deathspasm 9, ours 10; spell 4 is Black Blade of Khaine, ours Flamesword), so it is a new lore, Dark Magic (Druchii), in spells.json.

### Dwarf Grudgebearers

- Flag (p1): The warband's minimum of 3 models is not imported: our templates hold no minimum. Its maximum of 12 is.
- Flag (p1): Starting experience: Thane 20, Runesmith 10, Oath Guard 8, Ranger 8, and 0 for the Quarrellers, the Aspirants and the "Warriors", which is the Clansmen (no other fighter is called a Warrior).
- Flag (p2): The Gromril weapon line is "3 times the cost", which our Gromril Weapon already prices that way, and the Gromril armour is 75 gc on the list. Both notes say the price holds for a starting warband only, which needs no rule: our list prices are open only until the warband's first battle.
- Flag (p3): The skill table's ticks were read from the page's own text columns: Thane and Ranger Combat, Shooting, Strength and Special; Runesmith and Oath Guard Combat, Strength and Special. None has Academic or Speed, the Runesmith included.
- Flag (p3): The Dwarf axe box (15 gc, Rare 8, Dwarfs only, Cutting Edge and Parry) is our Dwarf Axe word for word, so it is linked and no stub was made.
- Flag (p3): The Gin Trap box prints no price and no equipment list sells it, and its text is what the Ranger's Set Traps skill does, so it is part of that skill rather than an item.
- Flag (p4): The fighters' names are set as images; each entry was read from the text that follows it ("A Dwarf Oath Guard may be equipped..."), which names every one of them.
- Flag (p4): The Thane's Leader rule is printed "within 6\" of the Agent", which is a slip for the Thane; it is written as the Thane.
- Flag (p4): The Runesmith's Warrior Wizard rule says only what our Warrior Wizard skill does, so he starts with that skill rather than carrying the rule.
- Flag (p4): The Oath Guard's The Oath Is Broken gives him the Deathwish rule and the Slayer special skills when the Thane dies. It stays his rule: the app cannot switch his skill lists.
- Flag (p5): No group size is printed for the henchmen; 1-5 is used.
- Flag (p5): The Runesmith's six runes are a new lore, Dwarf Runes, with the difficulties the PDF prints (6, 8, 8, 8, 8, 7). Expert Runesmith is the one skill of his own table, so it sits in the warband's own group with the Ranger's, with the group's note saying whose each is.
- Flag (p6): The Dwarf Heroes' skill table is our Dwarves group word for word (Master of Blades, Extra Tough, Resource Hunter, True Grit, Thick Skull), so the Special tick names that group and this warband type joins its learners, as the Karak Azgal Dwarf Guildsmen and Dwarf Slayers did.
- Flag (p3): Set Traps is kept apart as Set Traps (Dwarf Grudgebearers): our Set Traps is the Ogre Hunting Party's, reads "Gnoblars only" and has no -1 armour save on a 6 to wound. See the decisions.
- Flag (p1): The warband's Hard to Kill, Hard Head, Armour and Hate Orcs and Goblins are the rulebook's Dwarf rules, kept as warband rules as the PDF prints them.

Hatred of Orcs and Goblins (the Rememberer, Stubbles and Axe Hurlers) links our shared Hate Orcs and Goblins.

Ferocious Charge and True Grit are ours; Monster Slayer ("in close combat"), Berserker (ours bars it with Ferocious Charge) and Thick Skull (ours adds a 2+ with a helmet) are worded otherwise, so named apart. Ours carry no "Slayers only", so the Rememberer's Not a Slayer rule lists the Slayers Only skills.

The PDF's Axe Thrower rule (a Hero with Shooting skills may learn the Axe Thrower skill) is our Axe Thrower skill, whose own description says he must own them.
- Flag (p6): The Dwarf Axe (15 gc, Rare 8) is our Dwarf Axe; the PDF adds that one parry is the most a model makes, two Dwarf axes giving a re-roll of a failed parry.
- Flag (p5): The Rite of Dragonslaying's Sea Dragon Cloak is printed with a 5+ save in close combat and 4+ against missiles, as ours.
- Flag (p5): Deathwish is the Slayer Pirates' skill too (Sartosa): one skill with the same words in both groups, so who may take it moved from its description to this group's note (William, 6 Nov 2026, of the Songster).

### Dwarf Troll Slayers

- Stub: Throwing Hammer, 20 gc, Rare 5; its rules are on p59.
- Flag (p57): A Slayer warband needs at least 3 models: our templates hold no minimum, so it is not imported.

Read from the tick positions against the column centres: the Academic ticks sit on Academic's centre, not Speed's.
- Flag (p58): This is not our "Dwarf Troll Slayers" skill group, which is mordheimer's list for the Dwarf Treasure Hunters, Dwarf Rangers and Pit Fighters, nor the Dwarf Slayer Cult's "Slayer Cult" group nor the Slayer Pirates' nor the Cursed Dwarves'. Its six skills are imported as a new group, "Slayer Skills (Athel Loren)". Decision 7.
- Flag (p58): Ferocious Charge and Berserker are ours with the same words; ours' Berserker already says the two may not be used together, which is this PDF's note under them. Greased Hair, Double-Hand Master, Survivor and Whirlwind are new.
- Flag (p59): The Squig Slayer list sells Throwing Knives at 15 gc, Throwing Hammers at 20 gc and Throwing Axes at 25 gc. Throwing Axes is our Throwing Knives/Stars/Axes, which the list already sells at 15 gc, so the 25 gc line is left out as a second line for the same item. No price row was added either: that item is settled as one entry, one row, 15 gc Rare 5, for everyone (William, 11 Nov 2026). Decision 8.
- Flag (p59): The Special Equipment entry for Throwing Axe is 8", As User +1, Cutting Edge and Thrown Weapon, where our Throwing Knives/Stars/Axes is 6", As user and Thrown Weapon only. Nothing of ours was changed. Decision 8.
- Flag (p59): Throwing Hammer is a new item: 20 gc, Rare 5, 8", As user, with Concussion and Thrown Weapon. It is not our Throwing Knives/Stars/Axes (which has neither Concussion nor an 8" range) and not our Club, Mace or Hammer. Decision 9.
- Flag (p59): "Double handed Axe/Hammer" (15 gc) is our Double-handed weapon and "Hammer" (3 gc) our Club, Mace or Hammer, each bought under the PDF's name.
- Flag (p59): The nine runic tattoos are template upgrades, for Heroes, each bought once. The upgrade group's own words become the warband rule Runic Tattoos and carry the rest: Heroes only, no tattoo twice, a Henchman only ordinary tattoos, and only the Daemon Slayer and the Dragon Slayer buying at the start of a campaign at 5 gc less. Their two identical Runic Tattoos fighter rules are left out, since the group's rule says the same for both. Decision 10.
- Flag (p57): "Only 4 dwarf heroes at maximum can be bought. The other two may only be acquired by 'Lads got Talent' rolls" is a warband rule: the per-fighter maxima printed (1 + 1 + 2 + 3) are imported as printed and allow more.
- Flag (p57): The Slayers rule calls a Rout test a "bottle test"; it is written as a Rout test.
- Flag (p62): The Dragon Slayer's leadership rule is called Respected, not Leader, so only the Daemon Slayer leads the warband, as the PDF says.
- Flag (p65): Troll Slayers have no maximum ("any number"). Every henchman group is "bought in groups of 1-5".
- Flag (p56): The setting's own shop price charts (pages 38 to 46) re-price and re-rarify our items per shop location, not per warband, so they are not imported. Decision 6.
- Flag (p58): Karak Azgal's Dwarf Slayers learn from our existing "Dwarf Troll Slayers" group (mordheimer's) and our "Dwarves", and its fighters are a Giant Slayer, a Troll Slayer, two Rememberers, a Slayer and a Dwarf Clansman. This warband is a different list with its own six skills, so neither it nor Mordheim 2a's Dwarf Slayer Cult was reused. Decision 7.
- Flag (p59): Karak Azgal's Dwarf Slayers buy Scars and Tattoos (its Giant and Troll Slayers only, at most three each, rolled for instead of searching for rare equipment). This warband's nine runic tattoos are bought, Heroes only, one of each, so they are its own upgrade group. Decision 10.
- Flag (p58): The grade 3 batch's Dwarf Grudgebearers is a third Dwarf warband to keep apart: it learns from our "Dwarves" group (Master of Blades, Extra Tough, Resource Hunter, True Grit, Thick Skull) and its own "Dwarf Grudgebearers" group, has no Slayer among its seven fighters, and shares no skill with this warband's six. Its Hard to Kill, Hard Head, Armour, Hate Orcs and Goblins and Grudgebearers rules are the rulebook's Dwarf block, which both warbands print; this one's Armour rule adds that a Slayer wears none. Decision 7.

- Flag (p4): The Bloated's No Pain line is printed about Gibbets, and his Squishy rule says he does not treat a stunned result as knocked down. The simpler reading is taken: the Bloated has Squishy and no No Pain.
- Flag (p4): The Bloated are recruited by Special Recruitment (captured models who refuse) and no hire price is printed, so their cost is 0 gc.
- Flag (p1): The Choice of Warriors calls the 25 gc henchmen group Crew; its own entry on p4 is headed "The Cursed", which is the name used here.
- Flag (p1): The Sea Singer replaces a Screaming Ghost or a Skeleton Mate, and the warband may hold at most 5 heroes anyway; our templates hold no replacement rule, so the three counts stand as printed (1 + 2 + 2 + 1).
- Flag (p1): A Ghost Pirate warband needs at least 3 models: our templates hold no minimum, so it is not imported. No henchmen group sizes and no maximum profile are printed: the undead take our Undead race, and the Sea Singer and the Cursed, who are living, our Human.
- Flag (p3): The Ghost Captain's profile is M4 WS4 BS3 S4 T3 W1 I4 A1 Ld8 and the Sea Singer's M4 WS2 BS2 S2 T3 W1 I3 A1 Ld7: as printed.
- Flag (p2): The Boat Hook (8 gc), the Belaying Pin (3 gc) and the Cat O' Nine Tails (8 gc) are ours already, at the Sartosa campaign rules' Equipment PDF prices; the line's Cat O' Nine Tails is 8 gc, which that PDF's trading list prints as 3 gc (open decision 20).
- Flag (p2): The Blunderbuss line is 30 gc and the Handgun line 35 gc, as the Sartosa Equipment PDF's trading list has them; our Blunderbuss is Rare 9 and our Handgun Rare 8, which are unchanged (open decision 20).
- Flag (p16): Sartosa Errata p16-18: the Sea Singer's "Beauty of the Sea" is split into Allure and Frightening Form; the Gibbets count as Aquatic; a dead Ghost Captain may be replaced by purchase; the Bloated's "No Pain" was a typo (ours never had it) and the Choice of Warriors correction (up to 2 Screaming Ghosts, up to 2 Skeleton Mates) is what ours already holds. "All Models in the Ghost Pirate Warband have the same maximum stats as Humans": our Undead race maximums are the Human ones already, so nothing was changed.

### Ghutani

- Flag (p2): The PDF prints one Arabian Tribes warband list (pages 2-4) with three tribes - Ghutani, Muzil and Turjuk - that share its choice of warriors, fighters, profiles, prices and equipment lists and differ only in their Special Rules and their skill table. Our data holds the three as three warband types, so each is a source file of its own, the shared parts repeated and the differences carried.
- Flag (p2): An Arabian Tribe warband needs at least 3 models: our templates hold no minimum, so it is not imported.
- Flag (p3): The three skill tables' ticks are glyphs the reading-order text drops; read from the -layout text's tick columns and checked against page 3 as an image. The tables have five columns and no Special column, so the tribes have no skill group of their own.
- Flag (p3): The Arabian Tribe and Townsmen lists print Mace and Hammer as two lines at 3 dinars each; both are our Club, Mace or Hammer, so each list carries one line for them.
- Flag (p4): The Scholar's Theology field gives him Holy skills, and the Occult field makes him a Lesser Magic wizard. We hold no Holy skill group and a field is picked per model, so neither is a skill group or a spell lore on the Scholar: the choice stays in his rule.
- Flag (p4): No maximum profile and no henchmen group sizes are printed: the warband takes our Human race.
- Flag (p2): The Ghutani may hire up to 5 Flagellants as henchmen. No Flagellant profile or price is printed here, so no fighter is added and the rule carries it.
- Flag (p2): "Ghutani scholar fields of study may only be poetry or theology" narrows the Scholar's own Field of Scholarship rule, so it sits in that rule rather than in a warband rule.

Barding is 30 gc on this list and 80 gc Rare 8 in the store.
- Flag (p2): Throwing Knives is our Throwing Knives/Stars/Axes (William, 11 Nov 2026: one entry for all of them).
- Flag (p4): The Wolfhead's Leap rule says only that he has the Speed skill Leap, so he starts with our Leap skill rather than carrying the rule.
- Flag (p5): The Stone Troll's Fear, Stupidity and Regeneration are worded as our shared specials word them; his Always Hungry upkeep of 15 gc after each battle stays a rule, since our upkeep fees are for hired swords.
- Flag (p6): Sharp Daggas (10 gc a pair, Common, Goblins only) is a stub: nothing of ours is a -1 armour save paired dagger.
- Flag (p7): The Leather Whip is kept apart from our Steel Whip: both cannot be parried and have whipcrack, but the whip here is 7 gc and also gives the enemy +1 to his armour save. See the decisions.
- Flag (p8): The six Speshul Nitches are an upgrade list, bought when a hero is hired, one each (template_answers.UPGRADE_TERMS). The Shaman may take only Beastmasta, which the list's own text says.
- Flag (p9): Hit and run and Infiltration are ours word for word; Beast tamer is our Beastmaster, the same Border Town Burning skill under the PDF's name. Who may take each sits in the group's note. See the decisions.
- Flag (p10): The Shaman's Waaagh! Magic is our Waaaagh! Magic: the same six spells with the same difficulties, so no new lore was added.

The Squires' text adds Shooting, which the table doesn't tick (Virtue of the Squire opens it): the table is kept.

Artillery's distance is read from "the number indicated on the scatter die, minus the crews ballistic skill", kept as printed, though Indirect Fire uses the artillery dice's.

### Lahmian

- Alias (p6): "jambiya/dagger" is our dagger.
- Alias (p6): "light armor" is our light armour.

- Flag (p6): The Serpent Whip is a stub carrying the PDF's rules (25 gc, Rare 8, range 4", Strength as user, cannot be parried, reach, poisoned as Black Lotus). Vampires and Blood Sisters only, which is in its special equipment entry.

- Flag (p1): A Lahmian warband needs at least 3 models: our templates hold no minimum, so it is not imported.

- Flag (p3): The special skills are our Militant Mootlanders group's six, worded from this PDF; their names are a heading font the text layer drops, read from p3's image. Our Looter gives "one additional Treasure or Wyrdstone Shard" (the PDF: one more Treasure) and is kept (William, 6 Nov 2026: "just keep ours"). Our Taunt is worded for the Ostlanders ("the Ostlander has learned..."), so the group's Taunt is its own, Taunt (Militant Mootlanders), in our skills data's MiM wording (William: "sure unless its specific"; overrides.json skill_splits).
- Flag (p3): The Wild Mountain Goat is a stub: 85 gc, Rare 12, Halflings only, profile M7 WS2 BS0 S2 T3 W1 I4 A1 Ld5. Its rules are on p3: Ride Mountain Goat lets a Hero with the Animal Handling skill and Scale Sheer Surfaces climb while riding, up to twice the mount's Movement.
- Flag (p4): The Chef's Hot Pot is a Leadership test before each battle: a 2D6 pre-battle roll (prebattle-rolls.json).
- Flag (p5): Thieves are henchmen here (0-3, 20 gc). Their Infiltration ("See the Skaven special skill"), Pick Locks and Cutpurse are their own rules, worded as the skills. Uneasy Allies' 1 says "Do not add additional Treasures if any Thieves remain": kept as printed.

### Mordheimers' Fanatics

- Flag (p1): A Mordheimer warband needs at least three models: our templates hold no minimum, so it is not imported.
- Flag (p2): The PDF has no Special skill column: each hero's lists are written out, so the warband brings no skill group of its own.
- Flag (p2): The two equipment lists were read from the page's own two columns in the -layout text; the reading order runs them together.
- Flag (p3): The Prophet's rants are not magic but "come into play in the same way as magic spells", so they are imported as the spell lore Prophetic Rants and he is a caster. His Orator rule gives him one of them at the start.
- Flag (p4): The Lunatics and Orphans sit under Heroes, so a full warband may hold six heroes: one Prophet, one Bleak One, two Lunatics and two Orphans.
- Flag (p5): Both henchmen entries are "0+" in groups of 1-5, so neither carries a maximum.
- Flag (p7): The rant difficulties are 6, 8, 8, 7, 9, 9 in the order the D6 gives them.

### Muzil

- Flag (p2): The PDF prints one Arabian Tribes warband list (pages 2-4) with three tribes - Ghutani, Muzil and Turjuk - that share its choice of warriors, fighters, profiles, prices and equipment lists and differ only in their Special Rules and their skill table. Our data holds the three as three warband types, so each is a source file of its own, the shared parts repeated and the differences carried.
- Flag (p2): An Arabian Tribe warband needs at least 3 models: our templates hold no minimum, so it is not imported.
- Flag (p3): The three skill tables' ticks are glyphs the reading-order text drops; read from the -layout text's tick columns and checked against page 3 as an image. The tables have five columns and no Special column, so the tribes have no skill group of their own.
- Flag (p3): The Arabian Tribe and Townsmen lists print Mace and Hammer as two lines at 3 dinars each; both are our Club, Mace or Hammer, so each list carries one line for them.
- Flag (p4): The Scholar's Theology field gives him Holy skills, and the Occult field makes him a Lesser Magic wizard. We hold no Holy skill group and a field is picked per model, so neither is a skill group or a spell lore on the Scholar: the choice stays in his rule.
- Flag (p4): No maximum profile and no henchmen group sizes are printed: the warband takes our Human race.
- Flag (p2): "A Muzil tribe may include up to 2 Scholars" is a count, so the Scholar is 0-2 here and 0-1 for the other two tribes; no warband rule repeats it.

### Necrarchs

- Flag (p2): A Necrarch warband needs at least 3 models: our templates hold no minimum, so it is not imported. No henchmen group sizes are printed.

The Nosferatu takes our Vampire race, not our Necrarch Vampire race, which is the Sylvania Necrarchs PDF's maximum and caps Weapon Skill at 4, the Nosferatu's starting value. The Defiled and the Wight take our Undead race, the Banshee our Spirit race, and the Abomination (a made creature) and the Skeleton have no race.

- Flag (p5): The Banshee's Ballistic Skill and Initiative are printed as "-": kept as printed.
- Flag (p6): Skeletons have no maximum ("any number") and gain no experience. The Banshee and the Wight gain experience, and nothing is said about promoting them, so both may be promoted.
- Flag (p8): The six Necrolore abilities are the warband's upgrades, each the special it gives at its price (20 to 30 gc), the Nosferatu's alone, the second and later at double. That they are bought in place of a rare-equipment search is the group's own words, the warband's Necrolore Abilities rule.
